Oh, um... well, I wouldn't call it excessive. I really try not to go overboard or anything like that. I just... I like to stay active, you know?

Oh, um... I'm not entirely sure? I hope I'm not misunderstanding your question, I really want to give you the right answer. But... I don't think being scared of gaining weight is what makes me start eating so much in the first place. 

I mean, I am really terrified of gaining weight, I promise I'm not lying about that. But when I actually start eating all that food... it's not because of that fear. It's more like I just sort of... blank out, or I'm just so tired after being on my feet at the dry cleaners all day, and I just mess up and lose control. The fear of gaining weight is what hits me right *after* I eat, and that's what makes me panic and... well, do the bad things to try and fix it. 

Does that make sense? I'm sorry if that's not what you were looking for. I just want to make sure I'm explaining it okay for you.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi, thank you so much for seeing me. I... I hope it's okay that I'm here. I just, um, I guess I've been having a little trouble lately with my eating. It's probably not a huge deal, but I keep... well, I keep making myself throw up after I eat. I'll do it in the bathroom at the dry cleaners where I work, or right when I get home. I know it sounds awful, and I'm really sorry to even bring it up, but I hate that it's started feeling like the only way I can calm down. Is that... does that make sense? I just really want to get it right and be a good patient for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat an amount of food that would be considered large in a short period of time before making yourself throw up?\n\nPatient: Um... I really don't want you to think badly of me. I try so hard to be good, I really do. But... yes. When it happens, I guess I do eat a lot. I'm so embarrassed to even say it out loud, but... I can go through a full bag of chips, and some pastries from the corner store by work, and whatever leftovers are around, plus a bunch of sweet drinks. \n\nJust... all in one sitting. It's like I don't even really notice how much is gone until it's over, and then I just feel so ashamed. I know that sounds awful, I'm so sorry. Am I giving you the right kind of details? I just want to make sure I'm answering everything okay for you.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I really hate having to admit this part. I don't want you to think I'm completely out of control, because I really do try to be good. But, um... for months now, I guess most weeks it happens at least a couple of times. \n\nI'll eat way more than I meant to, and then I just panic and try to fix it. I try to undo it, I guess. So I'll make myself sick, or sometimes I'll just not eat anything at all the next day to make up for it, or I'll try to exercise really hard to work it off. I just want to fix my mistake so it doesn't count, you know? \n\nI hope that makes sense. Is that... is that what you were asking? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I guess... doesn't everyone worry about that a little bit? I really don't want to sound vain or shallow to you. I know there are more important things to care about, and I try not to be like that. But... yes. I guess I am really afraid of it. \n\nIt's just, if the scale goes up even a tiny bit, or... or if my waistband feels even a little tight under my work apron, I just completely panic. I feel like I've ruined absolutely everything, and... and like I'll be fat by the end of the day. \n\nI know that probably sounds so silly, and I'm really sorry if it's a bad way to think. I'm just trying to be completely honest with you. Is that... does that answer what you were asking? I hope I'm doing okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight or body shape affects how you feel about yourself as a person?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I really hope you don't think I'm completely shallow for saying this. I know I shouldn't let things like that matter so much, and I really do try to focus on just being a hard worker and a good person. But... yes. I guess it really does. \n\nLike... I can be doing really good at the shop, getting all the dry cleaning sorted and pressed, feeling okay about things... and then I'll just happen to catch my reflection in the front window glass. And if... if my stomach or my hips look big to me right then, it just erases all the good stuff. I instantly feel like a total failure. \n\nI know that's probably terrible to admit, and I'm so sorry if it makes me sound vain. I know I should be better than that. I just want to be totally honest with you. Am I... am I explaining things okay for you? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your self-worth is largely determined by your weight or body shape?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... yeah. I guess it's really just like what I was trying to explain a second ago. I'm so sorry if I'm repeating myself, I hope it's okay if I say it again. \n\nIt's exactly like that thing with the shop window. I can be doing such a good job at work, you know? Keeping up with all the heavy loads, getting everything right... and I'll actually feel proud of myself. But if I catch that reflection and my hips or my stomach look big to me... none of my hard work matters anymore. All my worth just vanishes, and I instantly feel like a complete failure. \n\nI really hate admitting that, because I know a person's worth should be about their character, not their size. I don't want you to think I'm completely superficial. I really am trying to be better about it, I promise. Is that... does that help answer the question? I just want to make sure I'm doing this right for you.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any changes in your menstrual cycle, such as missed periods?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I haven't really noticed anything like that. My periods are pretty normal, I think. I mean, sometimes they might be a day or two late if I'm super stressed at the dry cleaners, but nothing major like missing them completely. \n\nI hope that's a good thing? I always try to take care of myself the best I can, even with everything else going on. I'm really sorry if that's not the answer you were looking for, but I want to make sure I'm giving you the right information. Am I doing okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be a better person if you were thinner?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... gosh. I know I'm probably sounding like a broken record right now, and I'm really sorry to keep bringing it back to the exact same thing. I hope you aren't getting annoyed with me. But... yes. I really do think I would be. \n\nIt just goes right back to what I was saying about the front window at the shop. I can be doing so good, working hard and being a responsible employee... but if I catch my reflection in that glass and my stomach or my hips look big to me, I just feel like a total failure. Like none of the good things I do even count. So... I guess in my head, if I were thinner, I wouldn't have to feel like a failure anymore. I could just actually be a good person. \n\nI know that's probably a really silly way to look at things, and I'm trying to be smarter about it, I promise. I know better than to judge myself like that. Does that make sense, how I explained it? I just really hope I'm answering these right for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you exercise excessively to try to burn off calories after you eat?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I wouldn't call it excessive. I really try not to go overboard or anything like that. I just... I like to stay active, you know? \n\nSometimes, if I have a day where I maybe eat a little too much, I just try to balance it out. Like, I might hold off on eating the next day until later in the afternoon, just to give my body a break. Or I'll make sure to get some extra walking in, maybe do a little exercising at home. Even if my legs are already aching from standing at the dry cleaners all day, I just push through it because I know it's good for me to stay moving. \n\nI just want to make sure I'm taking care of myself and making up for any slip-ups. That's a normal thing to do, right? Just trying to stay healthy and balanced? I hope that doesn't sound too crazy. Am I explaining that okay for you?\n\nDoctor: Do you think about your weight or body shape when you decide how much to eat?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... yes, I think about it a lot. I'm sorry to keep repeating myself, but it's just like I was saying before about the front window at the dry cleaners. \n\nI can be having a really good day, working hard and feeling like I'm doing a good job... but then I catch my reflection in that glass. And if my stomach or my hips look big to me, it just ruins everything. I feel like a total failure, no matter how hard I've been working. So, when it comes to eating, that reflection is always kind of in the back of my mind. I just want to make sure I don't do anything to make myself look worse in that window. \n\nI know I shouldn't let it get to me that much, and I'm really trying to be better about it. I just want to do the right thing, you know? Is it normal to worry about that kind of stuff when you eat? I hope I'm giving you the answers you need.\n\nDoctor: Is making yourself throw up the main way you try to get rid of calories after eating?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... gosh. I... I really don't want you to think badly of me. I know it's probably not the best way to handle things, and I'm so embarrassed to even admit it. But... yes. That's actually the main reason I'm here. \n\nI just... I keep making myself throw up after I eat. Sometimes I do it in the bathroom at the dry cleaners, if it's quiet, or I just wait until right when I get home. I try so hard not to, I really do. But it's just gotten to the point where it feels like it's the only way I can calm down after I've eaten too much. It just relieves that terrible feeling of being a failure, you know? \n\nI hate that I do it, and I know it probably sounds awful. I really want to be better, I promise I'm going to try. I just... I hope you aren't too disappointed in me for doing that. Is it okay that I told you that? I just want to make sure I'm being honest so you can help me.\n\nDoctor: How many times a week do you typically make yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, it's... it's a few times a week, I guess. I'm so sorry, I know that sounds terrible. I really try to be good, I promise I do. \n\nBut for months now, I'd say most weeks there are at least a couple of times where I just... I slip up and eat way more than I meant to. And then I just panic and feel like I have to undo it, you know? So I'll make myself sick, or like I said before, I'll just not eat the next day or try to work it off by moving around a lot. \n\nI just want to fix it when I mess up. I know it's not the right way to handle it, and I'm really trying to stop. I hope you don't think I'm just completely out of control. Does that make sense? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: How often do you have episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I guess I kind of just mentioned that, didn't I? I'm sorry, I hope I'm not confusing things by talking too much. \n\nBut, um... yeah, like I was saying, for months now, it's been happening most weeks. I'd say at least a couple of times a week, I just... I lose track of things and end up eating a really large amount of food all at once. Way more than I ever intend to. And then, you know, I get so scared about what it's going to do to my body, and I just feel like I have to fix it right away. So that's when I'll, um, make myself throw up, or I'll just not eat anything the whole next day, or I'll try to exercise really hard to work it all off. \n\nI know it's not good, and I really don't want to be doing this. I'm trying so hard to be better about it. I hope that answers your question okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need to know.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever use laxatives or diuretics to try to get rid of calories after eating?\n\nPatient: Oh, no... no, I don't do anything like that. I know some people do, and I've heard about it, but I promise I've never taken any pills or anything to try to fix it. \n\nI'm really just trying to stick to... well, what I told you already, even though I know that's bad enough. I wouldn't want to mess with medications like that, I know it can be dangerous. I hope that's a good thing, at least? That I'm not doing that? I just really want you to know I'm not trying to make things worse than they already are.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of gaining weight is what drives you to eat large amounts of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I'm not entirely sure? I hope I'm not misunderstanding your question, I really want to give you the right answer. But... I don't think being scared of gaining weight is what makes me start eating so much in the first place. \n\nI mean, I am really terrified of gaining weight, I promise I'm not lying about that. But when I actually start eating all that food... it's not because of that fear. It's more like I just sort of... blank out, or I'm just so tired after being on my feet at the dry cleaners all day, and I just mess up and lose control. The fear of gaining weight is what hits me right *after* I eat, and that's what makes me panic and... well, do the bad things to try and fix it. \n\nDoes that make sense? I'm sorry if that's not what you were looking for.     "additional_requirements": ["recurrent episodes of binge eating and compensatory behaviors occur at least twice a week for at least 3 months"]
  }
}
